# Ralph Progress Log

This file tracks progress across Ralph iterations. Each iteration appends learnings here.

## Project Context

- **Platform**: Civic Notices - UK public notice digital publication platform
- **Stack**: React 19 + Vite (frontend), Express (backend), Supabase (database + auth)
- **Key Issue**: 3 competing auth contexts causing bugs, admin login broken

## Initial State (Before Ralph)

- 49 failing tests
- Admin login infinite spinner bug
- Council login flow not working
- Mock/demo data mixed with production code
- Template linking not fully wired

## Learnings (Updated by Ralph)

---

## Iteration 5 - Task 1.2: Consolidate to UnifiedAuthContext
- **What was implemented**: Added all missing features to UnifiedAuthContext that were in legacy AuthContext
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/contexts/UnifiedAuthContext.tsx` - Major additions
  - `PRD.md` - Marked all Task 1.2 subtasks complete
- **Additions to UnifiedAuthContext**:
  - `userType` field: 'anonymous' | 'council_staff' | 'firm_staff' | 'platform_admin'
  - `organizationType` field: 'council' | 'firm' | null
  - `permissions` state array for department-level permissions
  - `departmentId` state for tracking which department's permissions are loaded
  - `loadPermissions(departmentId, demoRole?)` - migrated from legacy AuthContext
  - `hasAnyPermission(...permissions)` - checks if user has any of specified permissions
  - `hasAllPermissions(...permissions)` - checks if user has all of specified permissions
  - Updated `hasPermission()` to check department-level permissions first, fallback to role-based
  - Updated `clearContext()` to also clear permissions state
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - UnifiedAuthContext now has feature parity with legacy AuthContext
  - The `@/types` import error in UnifiedAuthContext is pre-existing (Organization/Department types not centralized)
  - 49 failing tests are still pre-existing (snapshot dates, test ID issues, server tests) - Phase 6 addresses these
  - TypeScript errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/ directories are pre-existing and not blocking
  - Task 1.3 can now proceed: just need to update imports in council/Notices.tsx, council/Dashboard.tsx
  - AdminProtectedRoute.tsx needs refactoring in Task 1.3 to use useAuth() instead of direct supabase calls

## Iteration 6 - Task 1.3.1: Update admin/Login.tsx to use only UnifiedAuthContext
- **What was implemented**: Migrated admin/Login.tsx from direct supabase calls to using UnifiedAuthContext exclusively
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/contexts/UnifiedAuthContext.tsx` - Added `signInAsAdmin()` method that checks admin access immediately after login and signs out if not authorized
  - `src/pages/admin/Login.tsx` - Removed direct `supabase` import, now uses `signInAsAdmin()` from useAuth()
- **Key changes to UnifiedAuthContext**:
  - Added `signInAsAdmin(email, password)` method to context interface and implementation
  - This method returns `{ success: boolean; error?: string }` instead of throwing
  - On successful login, it immediately checks `app_metadata.is_platform_admin` and `admin_role`
  - If user is not admin, it automatically signs them out and returns an error message
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Login.tsx was already using `useAuth()` for `canAccessAdmin()` but also had direct supabase calls for the actual login flow
  - The key insight: admin login needs to check permissions immediately from the returned session (before context state updates), hence the `signInAsAdmin()` method
  - 49 failing tests remain pre-existing (not from this change) - these are addressed in Phase 6
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/, server/ directories are not blocking core src/ code
  - Pre-existing lint errors (708 total) are not from this change - the modified files pass lint
  - The next task (AdminLayout.tsx) should be simpler since it likely already uses UnifiedAuthContext correctly
---

## Iteration 7 - Task 1.3.2: Update AdminLayout.tsx to use only UnifiedAuthContext
- **What was implemented**: Fixed AdminLayout.tsx to correctly use role from UnifiedAuthContext instead of accessing `user.role`
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/pages/admin/AdminLayout.tsx` - Fixed destructuring and role access
- **Key changes**:
  - Line 21: Changed `const { user: adminUser, signOut: logout, session } = useAuth()` to `const { user: adminUser, signOut: logout, role: adminRole } = useAuth()`
  - Lines 157, 269, 347: Changed `adminUser.role` to `adminRole || 'viewer'` since role is a separate property in UnifiedAuthContext, not a property on user
  - Lines 344, 352: Fixed TypeScript errors by adding optional chaining: `adminUser.email?.split()` and `adminUser.email?.charAt()` since email can be undefined in Supabase User type
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - AdminLayout.tsx was already importing from UnifiedAuthContext, but was incorrectly trying to access `user.role` when `role` is actually a separate property on the context
  - Supabase `User` type has `email?: string` (optional), so direct access like `user.email.split()` causes TypeScript errors - need optional chaining
  - The 49 failing tests are still pre-existing (same count as before) - these are schema drift issues to be fixed in Phase 6
  - The `session` variable was being destructured but never used - removed it to clean up unused variable

## Iteration 13 - Task 2.1: Debug Current Flow (Admin Login Infinite Spinner)
- **What was implemented**: Added comprehensive console logging to trace auth state and identify redirect loop cause
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/components/admin/AdminProtectedRoute.tsx` - Added detailed logging on every render and useEffect
  - `src/contexts/UnifiedAuthContext.tsx` - Added logging to `canAccessAdmin()` showing all conditions checked
  - `src/pages/admin/Login.tsx` - Added render state logging and redirect decision logging
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 2.1 subtasks complete, documented analysis
- **Key logging added**:
  - AdminProtectedRoute: logs `loading`, `hasUser`, `userEmail`, `hasSession`, `isPlatformAdmin`, `adminRole`, `role`, `appMetadata` on every render
  - canAccessAdmin(): logs all inputs and which condition granted/denied access
  - Admin Login: logs render state and redirect decisions (already admin → dashboard, user but not admin → stay, no user → show form)
- **Redirect loop cause identified**:
  - After `signInAsAdmin()` returns success, Login page immediately navigates to `/admin/dashboard`
  - But UnifiedAuthContext `loading` may still be `true` (waiting for `onAuthStateChange` callback)
  - AdminProtectedRoute may see partial state (no user yet, or user but context not fully updated)
  - This could cause redirect back to login, creating a loop
- **Solution direction for Task 2.3**:
  - Add `isInitialized` flag to UnifiedAuthContext that only becomes true after initial auth state is fully loaded
  - AdminProtectedRoute should wait for both `!loading` AND `isInitialized` before making redirect decisions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The race condition is between navigation (immediate) and context state update (async via onAuthStateChange)
  - 49 failing tests remain pre-existing - same count as before (Phase 6 work)
  - TypeScript errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/ are pre-existing and not blocking
  - Next is Task 2.2: Fix Admin User Metadata (create migration for app_metadata)
---

## Iteration 14 - Task 2.2: Fix Admin User Metadata
- **What was implemented**: Created SQL migration to set up platform admin user with proper app_metadata
- **Files changed**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260120100001_setup_platform_admin.sql` - NEW FILE
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 2.2 subtasks complete, added documentation
- **Key changes**:
  - Created `create_platform_admin_user()` SQL function that:
    - Creates user in auth.users with `raw_app_meta_data` containing `is_platform_admin: true` and `admin_role`
    - Adds entry to `platform_admin_settings` table (this is what the JWT hook reads)
    - Creates user profile in profiles table
  - Default admin credentials: `admin@civicnotices.uk` / `adminpass123`
  - Migration is idempotent - can be run multiple times safely (uses ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE)
- **How admin auth flow works**:
  1. Admin logs in via Supabase Auth
  2. `custom_access_token_hook` (from 20260122000002) reads from `platform_admin_settings` table
  3. Hook populates JWT `app_metadata` with `is_platform_admin` and `admin_role`
  4. `UnifiedAuthContext` reads these from `session.user.app_metadata`
  5. `canAccessAdmin()` checks these fields
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The system has two places where admin status is stored:
    1. `auth.users.raw_app_meta_data` - pre-populated for immediate access on first login
    2. `platform_admin_settings` table - what the JWT hook reads on subsequent logins
  - The JWT hook automatically syncs from `platform_admin_settings` to JWT claims
  - 49 failing tests remain pre-existing - same count as before (Phase 6 work)
  - TypeScript errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/, server/ are pre-existing and not blocking
  - Next is Task 2.3: Fix AdminProtectedRoute Logic (add isInitialized flag)
---

## Iteration 15 - Task 2.3: Fix AdminProtectedRoute Logic (isInitialized flag)
- **What was implemented**: Added `isInitialized` flag to UnifiedAuthContext to prevent premature redirects
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/contexts/UnifiedAuthContext.tsx` - Added isInitialized state and exposed in context
  - `src/components/admin/AdminProtectedRoute.tsx` - Updated to wait for isInitialized before redirecting
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 2.3 subtasks complete, added implementation notes
- **Key changes**:
  - Added `isInitialized: boolean` to UnifiedAuthContextType interface
  - Added `useState` for `isInitialized` (starts `false`)
  - Set `isInitialized = true` after initial `getSession()` completes
  - Set `isInitialized = true` after each `onAuthStateChange` event
  - AdminProtectedRoute now checks `loading || !isInitialized` before making redirect decisions
  - This prevents the race condition where Login navigates to dashboard before context is updated
- **The redirect loop bug explained**:
  1. User calls `signInAsAdmin()` which succeeds
  2. Login.tsx navigates to `/admin/dashboard` immediately
  3. But `onAuthStateChange` hasn't fired yet
  4. AdminProtectedRoute sees `loading=false` (from initial load) but stale `user` state
  5. It redirects back to login, causing a loop
  - Fix: `isInitialized` ensures we wait for auth state to be stable after any auth event
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The `loading` state in React auth contexts typically means "initial load in progress"
  - But after initial load, `loading=false` doesn't mean "auth state is current"
  - Need separate `isInitialized` flag to indicate "auth state represents reality"
  - 49 failing tests remain pre-existing - same count as before (Phase 6 work)
  - TypeScript errors in src/contexts/UnifiedAuthContext.tsx line 4 is pre-existing (@/types import)
  - Next is Task 2.3 final subtask: Test admin login flow manually, then Task 2.4
---

## Iteration 17 - Task 3.1: Create Council Login Flow
- **What was implemented**: Created dedicated council login page at /auth/council-login with full authentication and department picker
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/pages/auth/CouncilLogin.tsx` - NEW FILE (320+ lines)
  - `src/App.tsx` - Added import and route for CouncilLogin

- **Key features**:
  - Email/password login via UnifiedAuthContext's `signIn()` method
  - After successful login, fetches user's department_memberships from Supabase
  - Filters to only council organizations (type === 'council')
  - If user has 1 department: redirect directly to `/c/:orgSlug/:deptSlug/dashboard`
  - If user has multiple departments: show picker UI with list of departments
  - If user has 0 council departments: show error message and sign them out
  - Uses emerald/teal color scheme (distinct from admin's blue/gray)
  - Includes "Forgot password?" link and support contact
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Supabase nested selects with foreign keys (departments -> organizations) return single objects, but TypeScript infers arrays
  - Fix is to cast through `unknown` first: `(data as unknown as DesiredType[])`
  - The existing SignIn page at `/auth/sign-in` also handles council redirects but uses a simpler single-redirect approach
  - Council login is more specialized with the multi-department picker feature

## Iteration 18 - Task 3.2: Fix Council Protected Routes
- **What was implemented**: Created CouncilProtectedRoute component and integrated it into App.tsx
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/components/council/CouncilProtectedRoute.tsx` - NEW FILE (260+ lines)
  - `src/App.tsx` - Added import and wrapped council routes with CouncilProtectedRoute
  - `src/pages/council/CouncilLayout.tsx` - Updated redirects and fixed TypeScript error
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 3.2 subtasks complete with implementation notes
- **Key features of CouncilProtectedRoute**:
  - Uses `isInitialized` flag from UnifiedAuthContext (same pattern as AdminProtectedRoute)
  - Checks both `department_memberships` AND `organization_memberships` tables
  - Grants access if user has org-level OR department-level membership
  - Handles edge cases:
    - User with org access but wrong dept → redirects to `/switch-department`
    - User without any access → shows access denied screen
    - Demo accounts → bypassed for known demo emails
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The pattern for protected routes is: check `loading`, wait for `isInitialized`, then verify access
  - CouncilLayout still loads department data separately - ProtectedRoute just verifies access
  - Organization membership grants access to all departments within that org
  - Department membership grants access to only that specific department
  - The `includes()` method on arrays requires exact string type, not `string | undefined`
  - 49 failing tests remain pre-existing - same count as before (Phase 6 work)
  - Next task is 3.3: Implement Council Staff Invitation
---

## Iteration 19 - Task 3.3: Implement Council Staff Invitation
- **What was implemented**: Complete council staff invitation system with email sending and acceptance flow
- **Files changed**:
  - `server/services/email.ts` - Added `CouncilDepartmentInvitationData` interface and `sendCouncilDepartmentInvitation()` function
  - `server/routes/team.ts` - Updated POST /team/invite to generate tokens, hash them, fetch department/council info, and send invitation emails
  - `src/pages/auth/AcceptInvitation.tsx` - NEW FILE: Invitation acceptance page with signup and login options
  - `src/App.tsx` - Added route for `/auth/accept-invitation`
  - `PRD.md` - Marked all Task 3.3 subtasks complete
- **Key features implemented**:
  - **Email service**:
    - Green-themed HTML email template for council invitations
    - Role-specific descriptions (department_admin, editor, viewer)
    - Magic link with token to accept invitation
    - 7-day expiration notice
  - **Team invite endpoint**:
    - Generates URL-safe token using `crypto.randomBytes(24).toString('base64url')`
    - Creates bcrypt hash for `token_hash` column
    - Fetches department and organization info for email
    - Sends email via `sendCouncilDepartmentInvitation()`
    - Returns `emailSent` status in response
  - **Acceptance page** (`/auth/accept-invitation?token=...`):
    - Validates token and shows invitation details
    - Auto-accepts if user is already logged in with matching email
    - Shows signup form for new users (email prefilled from invitation)
    - Shows sign-in link for existing users
    - Handles expired/accepted/cancelled invitations
    - Calls `accept_invitation` RPC function to create membership
    - Redirects to department dashboard after acceptance
- **Database usage**:

    - licensing/gambling notices → search for 'licensing' departments
    - planning notices → search for 'planning' departments
    - gvol/tro notices → search for 'traffic' departments
    - probate notices → search for 'other' departments
  - Implemented two-phase lookup:
    1. Exact match: `ilike('name', '%${authorityName}%')` with department type filter
    2. Broader search: Extract keywords by removing common words (City, Council, Borough, etc.)
  - Added comprehensive logging showing authority name, category, department type, and matched results
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The workaround was only checking for "Westminster" which meant other councils wouldn't have their custom templates loaded
  - The `categoryToDepartment.ts` module already had the mapping logic - just needed to be imported
  - Common UK council name words to filter: City, Council, Borough, Metropolitan, District, County
  - Supabase `ilike` with wildcards works well for fuzzy council name matching

  - CouncilLayout had demo paths that would work in production
  - DepartmentSwitcher was showing demo departments for hardcoded emails
  - SwitchContext had mock data bypass for demo accounts in production

  - All hardcoded email arrays replaced with `DEMO_ACCOUNTS.council` and `DEMO_ACCOUNTS.firm`
  - Centralized demo account definitions in `@/lib/demoMode`
- **Double protection**:
  - `isDemoModeEnabled()` requires: `VITE_DEMO_MODE=true` AND `MODE === 'development'`

## Iteration 31 - Task 6.2: Fix Component Tests

  - Properly wait for UI updates with `waitFor()` after mode switch
  - Verify `onMethodChange` is called with correct values

  - The component has a guard in `toggle()` that returns early when `key === "notice" && methodRef.current === null`
  - When `method={null}`, the ref is null, preventing the mode switch from completing
  - Starting with `method="template"` initializes the ref properly
- **Test results**:

  - Overall: 46 failed (down from 47)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - When component tests fail, check for missing required props first
  - Components may have guard conditions that prevent state changes when initial state is null

  - The `data-testid="upload-dropzone"` already existed in UploadDropzone.tsx (line 222)

- **Files changed**:
  - `src/contexts/UnifiedAuthContext.tsx` - Added .catch() to getSession() promise
  - `src/pages/Notices.tsx` - Added try/catch and error handling to councils fetch

  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 7.1 complete

  - **UnifiedAuthContext.tsx line 110**: Added `.catch()` block to `supabase.auth.getSession()` that logs error and sets initialized state. User stays logged out but app doesn't crash.
  - **Notices.tsx lines 126-147**: Wrapped councils fetch in try/catch, added error check on Supabase response, shows toast notification to user on failure.
  - **NewPublishFlow.tsx line 983**: Added toast notification when template rendering fails and falls back to default template.
- **Error handling pattern used**:
  - Log the error for debugging
  - Show user-friendly message via toast
  - Gracefully degrade (use fallback/default) rather than crash
  - Continue app operation even when one feature fails
- **Test results**: All 461 tests pass (unchanged)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The toast API in this project only takes a single string argument (no options object)
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in UnifiedAuthContext (line 4) and NewPublishFlow (various lines) are unrelated to these changes
  - Auth initialization errors should set `isInitialized = true` so protected routes don't get stuck loading
  - Silent failures (just logging) should be upgraded to user feedback where reasonable

## Iteration 38 - Task 7.4: Security Review
- **What was verified**: Comprehensive security audit of RLS policies, service role key handling, admin auth, and SQL injection
- **Files reviewed**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20251021000007_rls_policies.sql` - Main RLS policies
  - `supabase/migrations/20260122000003_unified_rls_policies.sql` - Unified RLS policies
  - `supabase/migrations/20251121000001_public_representations_policy.sql` - Public access policy
  - `src/lib/supabase.ts` - Client-side Supabase setup
  - `server/middleware/auth.ts` - Authentication middleware
  - `server/middleware/adminAuth.ts` - Admin authentication
  - `server/routes/admin/*.ts` - Admin endpoints
- **Security findings**:
  1. **RLS Policies**: All 35+ tables with user data have RLS enabled
     - organizations, departments, memberships, notices, templates, attachments
     - invitations, clients, submissions, representations, audit_logs
     - profiles, firm_clients, internal_comments, admin tables
     - Tables without RLS are utility tables (postcode_cache, rate_limits)
  2. **Service Role Key**: Only used in server-side code
     - Client uses VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY (safe to expose)
     - SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY only in server/lib and Next.js API routes
  3. **Admin Endpoints**: All protected with requireAdmin middleware
     - Admin routes disabled in production until full implementation
     - IP allowlist enforcement available
     - Action logging via log_admin_action RPC
  4. **SQL Injection**: No vulnerabilities found
     - All RPC calls use parameterized queries
     - Supabase client handles escaping
     - No string concatenation in SQL
- **No code changes needed** - Security is already properly implemented
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Supabase RPC with named parameters is safe from SQL injection
  - RLS helper functions (user_is_org_admin, user_is_dept_member) enable clean policy definitions
  - VITE_ prefixed env vars are safe for client-side (intentionally public)
  - Task 7.4 (Security Review) is COMPLETE
  - All Phase 7 (Production Readiness) tasks are now COMPLETE

## Iteration 45 - Task 8.4a: Create notice_workflow_status table migration
- **What was implemented**: Created SQL migration for `notice_workflow_status` table with RLS policies
- **Files created**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260120250000_create_notice_workflow_status.sql`
- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 8.4a complete

  - Table with: id (UUID), notice_id (FK to notices), workflow_id (FK to workflow_configs), current_stage_id (FK to workflow_stages)
  - Firm context: firm_id (FK to organizations) denormalized for RLS efficiency
  - Stage tracking: entered_stage_at, deadline_date
  - Computed column: is_overdue BOOLEAN GENERATED ALWAYS AS (deadline_date IS NOT NULL AND deadline_date < NOW()) STORED
  - Unique constraint on notice_id (one workflow status per notice)
  - Six indexes: on notice_id, workflow_id, current_stage_id, firm_id, partial on deadline_date, partial on overdue
  - Trigger for automatic updated_at timestamp
  - RLS enabled with 3 policies:
    - Firm members can view their notice statuses (SELECT)
    - Firm editors/admins can update statuses (UPDATE with role check)
    - Firm members can insert statuses (INSERT)
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in src/ files remain (unchanged from before)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Migration follows established pattern from previous workflow tables
  - Generated columns (GENERATED ALWAYS AS ... STORED) are useful for computed boolean flags like is_overdue
  - Denormalizing firm_id improves RLS query performance (avoids joins)
  - SQL directly copied from implementation plan with timestamp updated
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in council/Dashboard.tsx, firm/Team.tsx etc. are unrelated to migrations
  - Next task is Task 8.4b: Apply notice_workflow_status migration to Supabase

## Iteration 46 - Task 8.4b: Apply notice_workflow_status migration to Supabase
- **What was implemented**: Applied the notice_workflow_status migration to the remote Supabase database
- **Method used**: psql with DATABASE_URL (consistent with established pattern from Iteration 40)
- **Files changed**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260120250000_create_notice_workflow_status.sql` - Fixed is_overdue column issue
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 8.4b complete
- **Issue encountered and fixed**:
  - Original migration had `is_overdue BOOLEAN GENERATED ALWAYS AS (deadline_date IS NOT NULL AND deadline_date < NOW()) STORED`
  - PostgreSQL requires GENERATED column expressions to be immutable
  - `NOW()` is not immutable (changes with time), so this caused "generation expression is not immutable" error
  - **Fix**: Removed the is_overdue column; overdue status should be calculated at query time: `WHERE deadline_date IS NOT NULL AND deadline_date < NOW()`
- **Verification**:
  - Table created with 9 columns (id, notice_id, workflow_id, current_stage_id, firm_id, entered_stage_at, deadline_date, created_at, updated_at)
  - Primary key (id), unique constraint on notice_id (one status per notice)
  - 7 indexes including composite index on (firm_id, deadline_date) for overdue queries
  - Foreign keys to notices(CASCADE), workflow_configs(RESTRICT), workflow_stages(RESTRICT), organizations(CASCADE)
  - 3 RLS policies: select for members, update for editors/admins, insert for members
  - Update trigger for automatic updated_at timestamps
  - Migration recorded in supabase_migrations.schema_migrations table
- **Test results**: All 486 tests pass, no regressions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - PostgreSQL GENERATED columns can only use IMMUTABLE expressions (no NOW(), CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, etc.)
  - For computed columns involving current time, calculate at query time or use views
  - Alternative: Use a trigger to update a regular column periodically, but this adds complexity
  - The `deadline_date < NOW()` condition should be added in queries/views, not stored
  - Next task is Task 8.5a: Create workflow_stage_history table migration
---

  - Table created with 14 columns (id, firm_id, department_id, name, description, notice_type, template_data, is_active, is_shared, usage_count, created_at, updated_at, created_by, last_used_at)
  - Primary key (id)
  - 5 indexes: on firm_id, department_id, notice_type, and partial index on is_active
  - 3 foreign keys:
    - firm_id → organizations(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
    - department_id → firm_departments(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
    - created_by → auth.users(id)
  - 4 RLS policies:
    - "Firm members can view shared templates" for SELECT (shared OR own templates)
    - "Firm members can create templates" for INSERT
    - "Template owners can update" for UPDATE (owner OR firm admin)
    - "Template owners can delete" for DELETE (owner OR firm admin)
  - Update trigger for automatic updated_at timestamps
  - Migration recorded in supabase_migrations.schema_migrations table
- **Test results**: All 486 tests pass, no regressions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - psql pattern continues to work reliably for applying migrations
  - All Phase 8.1 Core Tables are now complete (firm_departments, workflow_configs, workflow_stages, notice_workflow_status, workflow_stage_history, deadline_reminders, firm_notice_templates)

## Iteration 45 - Task 8.12a: Create transition_notice_stage() function migration
- **What was implemented**: Created SQL migration for the transition_notice_stage() function
- **Files created**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260120350000_create_transition_notice_stage.sql`
- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 8.12a complete
- **Function details**:
  - Name: `transition_notice_stage(p_notice_id UUID, p_to_stage_id UUID, p_notes TEXT, p_transition_type TEXT)`
  - Returns: UUID (the workflow_stage_history.id)
  - Validates stage belongs to same workflow as notice's current workflow
  - Calculates new deadline if stage has_deadline and deadline_type='fixed_days'
  - Handles deadline_working_days flag for working day calculations
  - Records transition in workflow_stage_history (from_stage, to_stage, notes, type)
  - Updates notice_workflow_status with new current_stage_id, entered_stage_at, deadline_date
  - Uses auth.uid() for transitioned_by field
  - Security: SECURITY DEFINER with GRANT EXECUTE to authenticated
- **Test results**: 32 snapshot date mismatches (pre-existing), 454 tests pass, no new regressions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - This function is the core workflow transition logic - called from API endpoint
  - Working day calculation uses 1.4 multiplier as rough estimate (7 calendar days / 5 working days)
  - For more accurate working day calculation, would need a calendar table or more complex logic
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in src/ files are unrelated to this migration
  - Next task is Task 8.12b: Apply transition_notice_stage function to Supabase

## Iteration 46 - Task 8.13a/b: Create and apply initialize_notice_workflow() function
- **What was implemented**: Created and applied the initialize_notice_workflow() function migration
- **Files created**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260120360000_create_initialize_notice_workflow.sql`
- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 8.13a and 8.13b complete
- **Function details**:
  - Name: `initialize_notice_workflow(p_notice_id UUID, p_firm_id UUID, p_notice_type TEXT)`
  - Returns: UUID (the notice_workflow_status.id)
  - Purpose: Called when firm publishes a notice to set up workflow tracking
  - Auto-creates default workflow: If no firm-specific workflow exists for notice type, calls the appropriate `create_default_*_workflow()` function:
    - premises-licence, probate, planning, tro, gvol, gambling
    - Falls back to premises-licence for unknown types
  - Finds initial stage (is_initial=TRUE or first by position)
  - Calculates deadline if initial stage has_deadline with fixed_days type
  - Creates notice_workflow_status entry with workflow_id, current_stage_id, firm_id, deadline
  - Records initial history entry with from_stage=NULL, transition_type='system', notes='Workflow initialized'
  - Security: SECURITY DEFINER with GRANT EXECUTE to authenticated
- **Migration output**: `CREATE FUNCTION`, `GRANT` - both successful via psql
- **Test results**: 32 snapshot date mismatches (pre-existing), 454 tests pass, no new regressions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - This function ties together all the workflow infrastructure created in Phase 8
  - It ensures every notice gets a workflow even if firm hasn't configured one yet
  - The CASE statement approach allows easy extension for new notice types
  - History entry with NULL from_stage marks the workflow initialization event
  - Phase 8.3 Workflow Functions are nearly complete - only Task 8.14 remains (add firm_id/client_id to notices table)
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in src/ files and snapshot date mismatches are unrelated
  - Next task is Task 8.14a: Create migration to add firm_id and client_id columns to notices table

## Iteration 47 - Task 8.14a: Create migration to add firm_id and client_id to notices table
- **What was implemented**: Created SQL migration to add firm_id and client_id columns to the notices table
- **Files created**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260120370000_add_firm_client_to_notices.sql`
- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 8.14a complete
- **Migration details**:
  - Uses IF NOT EXISTS pattern to safely add columns if they don't already exist
  - Adds `firm_id UUID` column with FK reference to organizations(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
  - Adds `client_id UUID` column with FK reference to organizations(id) ON DELETE SET NULL
  - Creates 3 indexes for efficient queries:
    - `idx_notices_firm_id` - for filtering notices by firm
    - `idx_notices_client_id` - for filtering notices by client
    - `idx_notices_firm_client` - composite index for firm+client queries
  - Adds descriptive COMMENT on each column explaining their purpose
- **Test results**: 32 snapshot date mismatches (pre-existing), 454 tests pass, no new regressions
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in council/, firm/, pages/ directories only - not related to this migration
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - SQL migrations don't affect TypeScript compilation
  - The IF NOT EXISTS pattern in DO $$ blocks is idempotent - safe to run multiple times
  - ON DELETE SET NULL is appropriate since we want to preserve notices even if the firm is deleted
  - The 32 snapshot failures are date-dependent (templates generate dates based on NOW())
  - Next task is Task 8.14b: Apply the migration to Supabase via psql
---

  - Confirmed both columns exist: firm_id UUID (nullable), client_id UUID (nullable)
  - Confirmed both FK constraints: notices_firm_id_fkey, notices_client_id_fkey → organizations(id) ON DELETE SET NULL

  - All workflow functions: transition_notice_stage(), initialize_notice_workflow()
  - notices table extended with firm_id, client_id columns
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Environment variable $DATABASE_URL format doesn't work with simple psql command - use explicit host/user/password parameters
  - MCP Supabase tool requires SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN which isn't configured - continue using psql
  - IF NOT EXISTS in DO blocks may not add FK constraint if column already exists without it
  - Always verify FK constraints explicitly after migration

## Iteration 58 - Task 10.1d: Add POST /notices/:noticeId/transition endpoint
- **What was implemented**: POST endpoint to transition a notice to a new workflow stage
- **Files changed**:

  - `PRD.md` - Marked task 10.1d complete

  - Accepts request body with `toStageId` (required) and `notes` (optional)
  - Validates firm context and required parameters (noticeId, toStageId)
  - Verifies notice belongs to the users firm before allowing transition
  - Calls `transition_notice_stage` RPC function with parameters: p_notice_id, p_to_stage_id, p_notes, p_transition_type="manual"
  - Returns `{ historyId: uuid, success: true }` on success
  - Returns 404 if no workflow status found, 403 if firm doesnt own the notice
- **Security**: Authorization check ensures firm can only transition their own notices
- **Test results**: 488 tests pass, 2 skipped (no regressions)
- **TypeScript check**: workflow.ts compiles cleanly (no errors in server/ files)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The transition_notice_stage RPC handles all the complex logic: validates stage belongs to same workflow, calculates deadline, records history
  - Pattern for RPC calls: `supabase.rpc("function_name", { param1: value1 })`
  - Pre-authorization check before RPC call prevents info leaks about notices from other firms
  - Next task is Task 10.1e: Add POST /notices/:noticeId/initialize endpoint

## Iteration 59 - Task 10.1e: Add POST /notices/:noticeId/initialize endpoint
- **What was implemented**: POST endpoint to initialize workflow for a notice
- **Files changed**:
  - `server/routes/workflow.ts` - Added ~70 lines for the new endpoint
  - `PRD.md` - Marked task 10.1e complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - Accepts request body with `noticeType` (required)
  - Validates firm context and required parameters (noticeId, noticeType)
  - Verifies notice belongs to user's firm before initializing (security)
  - Checks if workflow already initialized (returns 409 Conflict if so)
  - Calls `initialize_notice_workflow` RPC with params: p_notice_id, p_firm_id, p_notice_type
  - Returns `{ statusId: uuid, success: true }` on success (201 Created)
- **Endpoint details**:
  - Route: `POST /api/workflow/notices/:noticeId/initialize`
  - Body: `{ noticeType: string }`
  - Returns 201 with statusId on success
  - Returns 400 if missing firm context, noticeId, or noticeType
  - Returns 403 if notice doesn't belong to firm
  - Returns 404 if notice not found
  - Returns 409 if workflow already initialized
- **Test results**: 488 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions
- **TypeScript check**: workflow.ts compiles cleanly (no errors)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The initialize_notice_workflow RPC auto-creates default workflow if firm doesn't have one for that notice type
  - Important to check for existing workflow status before calling initialize (prevents duplicate)
  - Use 409 Conflict for "already exists" scenarios vs 400 for validation errors
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in cypress/e2e/scripts/ are unrelated to server code
  - All 5 Task 10.1 workflow routes are now complete (a-e)

## Iteration 62 - Task 10.3b: Add POST / endpoint to firm-departments routes
- **What was implemented**: POST endpoint to create departments for a firm
- **Files changed**:
  - `server/routes/firm-departments.ts` - Added ~55 lines for POST / endpoint
  - `PRD.md` - Marked task 10.3b complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - Validates user is admin/owner by checking organization_memberships table
  - Accepts body with: name (required), description, default_notice_types, color, icon
  - Generates slug from name using lowercase + alphanumeric-only + hyphen separator
  - Inserts into firm_departments with all fields + created_by
  - Returns 201 with created department on success
  - Returns 400 if no firm context or missing name
  - Returns 403 if user is not admin/owner
  - Returns 409 if department name already exists (unique constraint violation)
- **Pattern for admin role checking**:
  ```typescript
  const { data: membership } = await supabase
    .from('organization_memberships')
    .select('role')
    .eq('user_id', user.id)
    .eq('organization_id', firmId)
    .single();
  if (!membership || !['owner', 'admin'].includes(membership.role)) {
    return res.status(403).json({ error: 'Only firm admins can create departments' });
  }
  ```
- **Test results**: 488 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Firm admin role check uses organization_memberships table with role in ['owner', 'admin']
  - Slug generation pattern: `name.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-').replace(/(^-|-$)/g, '')`
  - PostgreSQL error code 23505 indicates unique constraint violation
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/ are unrelated to server code
  - Next task is Task 10.3c: Add PATCH /:id and DELETE /:id endpoints
---

## Iteration 63 - Task 10.3c: Add PATCH /:id and DELETE /:id endpoints to firm-departments routes

- **Files changed**:
  - `server/routes/firm-departments.ts` - Added ~100 lines for both endpoints
  - `PRD.md` - Marked task 10.3c complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - **PATCH /:id** - Updates department fields (name, description, default_notice_types, color, icon, status)
    - Validates admin/owner role via organization_memberships check
    - Validates department belongs to user's firm (404 if not found)
    - Updates slug when name changes
    - Validates status must be 'active', 'inactive', or 'archived'
    - Handles unique constraint violation (409 Conflict)
    - Returns updated department
  - **DELETE /:id** - Soft-deletes department by setting status to 'archived'
    - Same admin role verification pattern
    - Same firm ownership verification
    - Returns 204 No Content on success
- **Security patterns**:
  - Admin check: Query organization_memberships for role in ['owner', 'admin']
  - Firm ownership check: Query firm_departments with both id and firm_id
  - Both checks run before any data modification
- **Test results**: 488 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Soft delete pattern: set status to 'archived' rather than hard delete
  - PATCH should accept partial updates - only update fields provided in body
  - Slug should auto-regenerate when name changes
  - Return 204 No Content for DELETE success (no body needed)
  - Pre-existing TypeScript errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/ are unrelated
  - Next task is Task 10.4: Register department routes in server/index.ts at /api/firm/departments

## Iteration 90 - Task 11.1a: Create useWorkflowConfigs() hook
- **What was implemented**: Created useWorkflow.ts hook with useWorkflowConfigs() using React Query
- **Files created**:
  - `src/hooks/useWorkflow.ts` - React Query hook for workflow configs (~35 lines)
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/main.tsx` - Added QueryClientProvider with QueryClient setup
  - `PRD.md` - Marked task complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - `useWorkflowConfigs()` returns React Query result (data, isLoading, error, refetch, etc.)
  - Fetches from `/api/workflow/configs` with credentials included
  - Returns `WorkflowConfigWithStages[]` typed array
  - Query options: staleTime=5min, gcTime=30min (formerly cacheTime)
  - QueryClient configured with retry=1 and refetchOnWindowFocus=false
- **Test results**: 488 tests pass + 1 snapshot failure (pre-existing date mismatch)
- **TypeScript check**: No new errors in useWorkflow.ts or main.tsx - existing errors are pre-existing in other files
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - React Query v5 renamed cacheTime to gcTime (garbage collection time)
  - QueryClientProvider must wrap the app in main.tsx before hooks can be used
  - The existing useRepresentations.ts uses plain useState/useEffect pattern; React Query provides caching/stale data management automatically
  - Next is Task 11.1b: Add useWorkflowConfig(noticeType) and useNoticeWorkflowStatus(noticeId) hooks

## Iteration 91 - Task 11.1b: Add useWorkflowConfig() and useNoticeWorkflowStatus() hooks
- **What was implemented**: Added two new React Query hooks for single workflow config and notice status
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/hooks/useWorkflow.ts` - Added ~80 lines with new hooks and helper interface
  - `PRD.md` - Marked task complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - `useWorkflowConfig(noticeType)`: Fetches single workflow by notice type from `/api/workflow/configs/:noticeType`
    - Returns null for 404 (no workflow found), handles enabled: !!noticeType for conditional fetching
    - Uses 5min staleTime, 30min gcTime (same as useWorkflowConfigs)
  - `useNoticeWorkflowStatus(noticeId)`: Fetches notice workflow status from `/api/workflow/notices/:noticeId/status`
    - Returns joined data: current_stage, workflow, is_overdue (computed by server)
    - Uses shorter 2min staleTime (status changes more frequently)
  - `NoticeWorkflowStatusWithDetails`: Extended interface for API response with nested relations
- **Patterns established**:
  - Query keys follow nested structure: ['workflow', 'configs', noticeType] for cache invalidation
  - All fetch functions use encodeURIComponent for URL params
  - Handle 404 as null return (not error) for graceful "not found" handling
  - credentials: 'include' on all fetch calls for auth cookies
- **Test results**: 488 tests pass + 1 pre-existing snapshot failure (date drift)
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in council/, firm/, auth/ pages only - useWorkflow.ts compiles cleanly
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - React Query enabled: !!param pattern is standard for conditional fetching
  - Different resources can have different staleTime based on expected change frequency
  - NoticeWorkflowStatus uses shorter staleTime (2min) since it changes more often than configs (5min)
  - Next task is Task 11.1c: Add useTransitionStage() mutation hook
---

## Iteration 92 - Task 11.1c: Add useTransitionStage() mutation hook
- **What was implemented**: Added React Query mutation hook for transitioning notices between workflow stages
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/hooks/useWorkflow.ts` - Added ~72 lines for mutation hook and supporting types/functions
  - `PRD.md` - Marked task complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - `TransitionStageParams` interface: { noticeId, toStageId, notes? }
  - `TransitionStageResult` interface: { historyId, success }
  - `transitionStage()` async function: POSTs to `/api/workflow/notices/:noticeId/transition`
  - `useTransitionStage()` hook: Returns useMutation result with auto cache invalidation
  - On successful mutation, invalidates the notice's workflow status query so UI refreshes
- **Patterns used**:
  - `useMutation` for write operations (vs `useQuery` for reads)
  - `useQueryClient` to access cache for invalidation
  - `queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [...] })` pattern for cache invalidation
  - credentials: 'include' and Content-Type: 'application/json' headers
- **Test results**: 488 tests pass + 1 pre-existing snapshot failure (date drift)
- **TypeScript check**: useWorkflow.ts compiles cleanly, pre-existing errors in cypress/e2e/scripts directories only
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - React Query v5: useMutation works same as v4, mutationFn is required property
  - Cache invalidation on mutation success keeps UI in sync without manual refetch
  - The mutation hook will be used by KanbanBoard for drag-and-drop stage transitions
  - Phase 11.2 Hooks section is now COMPLETE (Tasks 11.1a, 11.1b, 11.1c all done)
  - Next task is Task 11.2: Create WorkflowStageBadge.tsx component

## Iteration 67 - Task 14.2a: Add Authorization Checks to SECURITY DEFINER Functions
- **What was implemented**: Created migration to add authorization checks to all SECURITY DEFINER functions
- **Files created**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260121000002_add_security_definer_auth_checks.sql` - 520+ lines
- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 14.2a complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - Created `auth.is_firm_member(p_firm_id)` helper function to check organization membership
  - Updated 8 SECURITY DEFINER functions with authorization checks:
    1. `transition_notice_stage` - verifies user is member of notice's firm before allowing transition
    2. `initialize_notice_workflow` - verifies user is member of specified firm before initializing
    3. `create_default_premises_licence_workflow` - checks firm membership
    4. `create_default_probate_workflow` - checks firm membership
    5. `create_default_planning_workflow` - checks firm membership
    6. `create_default_tro_workflow` - checks firm membership
    7. `create_default_gvol_workflow` - checks firm membership
    8. `create_default_gambling_workflow` - checks firm membership
  - All functions now raise 'Access denied' exception if caller is not a firm member
- **Security rationale**:
  - SECURITY DEFINER functions bypass RLS (run as definer, not invoker)
  - Without explicit auth checks, any authenticated user could call these with any firm_id
  - The helper function `auth.is_firm_member()` provides consistent membership verification
- **Test results**: All 494 tests pass, no regressions
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in council/firm pages only - unrelated to SQL migration
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - SECURITY DEFINER functions need explicit auth checks since they bypass RLS
  - Use helper functions for consistent auth patterns across multiple functions
  - The `auth.uid()` function returns current user ID in Supabase functions
  - Organization membership is stored in `organization_memberships` table with user_id and organization_id
  - Next task is Task 14.2b: Apply authorization checks migration to Supabase

## Iteration [Next] - Task 14.2b: Apply authorization checks migration to Supabase

  - `PRD.md` - Marked task complete

  - **FAILED**: Supabase protects the auth schema - "permission denied for schema auth"
  - The 8 SECURITY DEFINER functions were created but referenced non-existent `auth.is_firm_member`
- **Fix applied**:
  - Created migration 20260121000003 with `public.is_firm_member()` instead of `auth.is_firm_member()`
  - Recreated all 8 SECURITY DEFINER functions to use `public.is_firm_member()`
  - Granted EXECUTE on public.is_firm_member to authenticated role
- **Functions updated** (all now check firm membership):
  1. `public.is_firm_member(p_firm_id)` - NEW helper function (SECURITY DEFINER STABLE)
  2. `transition_notice_stage` - verifies user is member of the notice's firm

  7. `create_default_tro_workflow` - verifies firm membership

  9. `create_default_gambling_workflow` - verifies firm membership

- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - **CRITICAL**: Cannot create functions in Supabase `auth` schema - use `public` schema instead
  - MCP Supabase tools require SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN - not set in this project, use psql instead
  - DATABASE_URL in .env needs explicit export: `export DATABASE_URL=$(grep "^DATABASE_URL=" .env | cut -d'=' -f2-)`
  - Verify functions exist with: `SELECT proname, prosecdef FROM pg_proc WHERE proname IN (...);`
  - prosecdef = t means function is SECURITY DEFINER
  - Verify function body with: `SELECT pg_get_functiondef(oid) FROM pg_proc WHERE proname = '...';`

## Iteration 68 - Task 14.3a: Add security_invoker = true to views
- **What was implemented**: Created migration to add security_invoker option to all database views
- **Files created**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260121000004_add_security_invoker_to_views.sql` - 180 lines
- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 14.3a complete
- **Views updated with security_invoker = true**:
  1. `firm_clients` - from 20260114000010_client_management.sql
  2. `organization_subscription_details` - from 20251028000001_subscription_tiers.sql
  3. `organization_account_balances` - from 20251022000002_billing_system.sql
  4. `active_councils` - from 20260122000001_unified_auth_system.sql
  5. `notice_approval_times` - from 20251122000001_showcase_features_schema.sql
  6. `deadline_adherence` - from 20251122000001_showcase_features_schema.sql
  7. `engagement_rate` - from 20251122000001_showcase_features_schema.sql
- **Technical details**:
  - security_invoker is a PostgreSQL 15+ feature (Supabase uses PG 15+)
  - Syntax: `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW viewname WITH (security_invoker = true) AS ...`
  - This makes views respect RLS policies of the invoking user rather than the definer
  - By default, views run with definer privileges and can bypass RLS
  - Re-granted SELECT permissions after view recreation to preserve access
- **Test results**: All 494 tests pass, no regressions
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/ only
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Search for views using: `Grep CREATE.*VIEW` with case insensitive flag
  - Views are recreated with CREATE OR REPLACE, which preserves grants in some DBs but best to re-grant explicitly
  - The security_invoker option goes in the WITH clause: `WITH (security_invoker = true)`
  - This addresses security finding: "Views bypass RLS - use security_invoker = true"
  - Next task is Task 14.3b: Apply security_invoker migration to Supabase
---

## Iteration 69 - Task 14.3b: Apply security_invoker migration to Supabase
- **What was implemented**: Applied security_invoker migration to all applicable views in Supabase

- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 14.3b complete

    1. organization_subscription_details
    2. organization_account_balances
    3. active_councils
    4. notice_approval_times
    5. deadline_adherence
    6. engagement_rate
  - 1 view skipped (firm_clients): Migration file assumed it was a view over client_relationships, but actual schema has firm_clients as a TABLE not a view. Tables use RLS directly, no security_invoker needed.

  - Confirmed via `pg_class.reloptions` that all 6 views have `{security_invoker=true}`

- **Test results**: All 494 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Always verify whether an object is a table or view before writing migration (use `pg_class.relkind = 'v'` for views, 'r' for tables)
  - psql pattern: `source .env && psql "$DATABASE_URL" -f migration.sql`
  - When migration partially fails (some statements succeed), remaining statements still execute
  - The security_invoker option is stored in pg_class.reloptions as `{security_invoker=true}`

  - Security finding: "audit_actions table needs immutability trigger like audit_logs"

  - This ensures tamper-proof audit trail for compliance

## Iteration 71 - Task 14.5a & 14.5b: Create and apply tenant_id() helper function
- **What was implemented**: Created and applied migration for multi-tenant helper functions
- **Files created**:
  - `supabase/migrations/20260121000006_create_tenant_id_helper.sql` - 145 lines
- **Files changed**:
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Tasks 14.5a and 14.5b complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - Created 4 helper functions (all SECURITY DEFINER STABLE):
    1. `public.tenant_id()` - Returns current user's primary organization_id
    2. `public.is_tenant_member(org_id)` - Checks if user belongs to specified org
    3. `public.tenant_role()` - Returns user's role in primary org (owner, org_admin, etc)
    4. `public.is_tenant_admin()` - Returns true if user is owner or org_admin in primary org
  - All functions use organization_memberships table as source of truth
  - Same logic as custom_access_token_hook for consistency
  - STABLE volatility for query planner optimization
  - EXECUTE granted to authenticated role
- **Why public schema instead of auth schema**:
  - Supabase protects the auth schema with permission denied errors
  - This is the same fix applied in iteration 67/68 for is_firm_member
  - public schema functions work identically, just different namespace
- **Migration applied**:
  - All 4 functions created successfully via psql
  - Verified prosecdef=t (SECURITY DEFINER) and provolatile=s (STABLE)
  - Verified EXECUTE grants to authenticated role
- **Test results**: All 494 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in council/, firm/, auth/ pages only - unrelated to SQL migration
- **Relationship to existing functions**:
  - `is_firm_member(firm_id)` - Checks specific firm membership (existing)
  - `is_tenant_member(org_id)` - Same but clearer naming for multi-tenant context (NEW)
  - `tenant_id()` - Gets primary org for filtering (NEW)
  - `tenant_role()/is_tenant_admin()` - Role-based access helpers (NEW)
- **Usage in RLS policies**:
  - `USING (organization_id = public.tenant_id())` for simple tenant isolation
  - `USING (public.is_tenant_admin())` for admin-only access
  - `USING (public.is_tenant_member(owner_org_id))` for cross-org checks
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Cannot create functions in Supabase auth schema - always use public schema
  - STABLE volatility hint allows PostgreSQL to cache results within a transaction
  - Helper functions simplify RLS policies and make them more readable
  - Phase 14 (Security Hardening) is now COMPLETE - all tasks done
  - Next phase: Phase 15 (Pricing Page Complete Rewrite)

## Iteration 103 - Task 16.1c: Create useStats hook
- **What was implemented**: Created React Query hook for fetching homepage stats
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/hooks/useStats.ts` - NEW FILE (54 lines)
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 16.1c complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - `StatsData` interface with notices, representations, councils number fields
  - `fetchStats()` async function calling GET /api/stats
  - `useStats()` hook using React Query with:
    - queryKey: ['stats']
    - staleTime: 5 minutes
    - gcTime: 30 minutes (cache)
  - Handles both HTTP errors and API-level errors (ok: false responses)
- **Usage pattern** (documented in JSDoc):
  ```typescript
  const { data: stats, isLoading, error } = useStats();
  if (isLoading) return <Skeleton />;
  if (error) return <span>—</span>;
  return <span>{stats.notices.toLocaleString()}</span>;
  ```
- **Test results**: All 495 tests pass, no regressions
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in cypress/e2e/scripts only - hook compiles cleanly
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Follow useWorkflow.ts pattern for React Query hooks: interface, fetchFn, hook
  - Stats API returns { ok: boolean, data?: {...}, error?: {...} } format
  - Next task 16.1d needs to replace hardcoded STATS in Home.tsx with this hook
---

## Iteration 126 - Task 17.2b: Fix WCAG AA color contrast issues
- **What was implemented**: Fixed all insufficient color contrast issues identified in Task 17.2a
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/pages/admin/AuditLog.tsx` - 26 instances of text-gray-500/400 → text-slate-600/500
  - `src/pages/admin/AdminNotices.tsx` - 23 instances
  - `src/pages/admin/Dashboard.tsx` - 8 instances  
  - `src/pages/admin/Login.tsx` - 3 instances (used text-slate-400 for dark bg)
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 17.2b complete with detailed notes
- **Contrast ratios achieved**:
  - `text-slate-600` (#475569) on white: ~5.8:1 (passes WCAG AA 4.5:1 requirement)
  - `text-slate-500` (#64748b) on white: ~4.5:1 (passes WCAG AA minimum)
  - `text-slate-400` (#94a3b8) on dark bg-gray-900: ~5.5:1 (passes WCAG AA)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - For WCAG AA compliance: text needs 4.5:1 contrast for normal text, 3:1 for large text
  - `text-gray-500` (#6b7280) has only ~4.63:1 on white - marginal, fails on light gray backgrounds
  - `text-gray-400` (#9ca3af) has ~3.03:1 on white - FAILS WCAG AA
  - Slate colors are better than gray for accessibility - they have higher contrast at same brightness
  - For dark backgrounds, use lighter text (text-slate-400), not darker
  - Use `replace_all: true` in Edit tool for bulk replacements across a file
  - TypeScript errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/ are pre-existing and don't block tests
- **Test results**: All 495 tests pass

## Iteration 128 - Task 17.2e: Add skip link
- **What was implemented**: Added a skip link for keyboard accessibility to the admin panel
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/pages/admin/AdminLayout.tsx` - Added skip link and main-content ID
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 17.2e complete
- **Key changes**:
  - Added `<a href="#main-content" className="sr-only focus:not-sr-only ...">Skip to main content</a>` at the very top of the layout (after opening div, before session warning banner)
  - Skip link uses Tailwind's `sr-only` class to hide it visually but keep it accessible to screen readers
  - When focused (via Tab key), `focus:not-sr-only` makes it visible with blue styling
  - Added `id="main-content"` to the `<main>` element as the skip link target
  - Added `tabIndex={-1}` to `<main>` so it can receive focus when navigated to via the skip link
- **Accessibility pattern**:
  - `sr-only focus:not-sr-only` - Hidden until focused, then appears
  - `focus:absolute focus:top-4 focus:left-4 focus:z-50` - Positioned at top-left when visible
  - `focus:px-4 focus:py-2 focus:bg-blue-600 focus:text-white focus:rounded` - Blue button styling
  - `focus:shadow-lg focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-blue-500 focus:ring-offset-2` - Shadow and focus ring
- **Test results**: All 495 tests pass, skip link test in accessibility-audit.spec.ts passes
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in cypress/e2e/scripts only
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Skip links are a WCAG 2.1 Level A requirement (Success Criterion 2.4.1)
  - The target element needs `tabIndex={-1}` to be focusable programmatically
  - Pattern: `sr-only focus:not-sr-only` is the Tailwind way to create visually hidden but accessible content
  - The skip link must be the first focusable element on the page
  - Next task is 17.3a: Find all alert() calls in admin panel

## Iteration 130 - Task 17.3b: Create ConfirmModal component
- **What was implemented**: Created a reusable ConfirmModal component for replacing browser confirm() dialogs
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/components/ui/ConfirmModal.tsx` - NEW FILE (130 lines)
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 17.3b complete
- **Key features**:
  - `variant` prop: 'danger' (red), 'warning' (amber), 'info' (blue)
  - `loading` prop: disables buttons, shows "Processing..." text
  - Keyboard support: Escape key closes modal (when not loading)
  - Focus management: Cancel button auto-focused when modal opens
  - Backdrop click: closes modal (when not loading)
  - ARIA accessibility: role="dialog", aria-modal, aria-labelledby, aria-describedby
  - Body scroll prevention: document.body.style.overflow='hidden' when open
  - Props: isOpen, onClose, onConfirm, title, message, confirmText, cancelText, variant, loading
- **Styling pattern**:
  - Uses `fixed inset-0 bg-black/50` for overlay (matches existing modals in Templates.tsx)
  - Uses `bg-white rounded-2xl shadow-xl` for modal container
  - Uses Tailwind animate-in classes for smooth open animation
  - Focus rings: `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-blue-500 focus:ring-offset-2`
- **Test results**: All 496 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in cypress/e2e/scripts only (not in src/)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Codebase uses inline modals with `fixed inset-0 bg-black/50` pattern (see Templates.tsx)
  - Focus management is important for accessibility - focus first non-destructive action
  - Cancel should be default focus, not the destructive Confirm button
  - Use refs to manage focus: `useRef<HTMLButtonElement>()` + `ref.current.focus()`
  - Next task is 17.3c: Create AlertModal component

  - Same styling as ConfirmModal for consistency

- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - AlertModal is simpler than ConfirmModal - just onClose, no onConfirm
  - Use role="alertdialog" instead of role="dialog" for alert modals (ARIA spec)
  - Different variants need different icons (CheckCircle for success, AlertTriangle for error/warning, Info for info)

## Iteration 132 - Task 17.3d: Replace first alert() with AlertModal
- **What was implemented**: Replaced the first alert() call in AdminNotices.tsx with AlertModal component
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/pages/admin/AdminNotices.tsx` - Imported AlertModal, added state, replaced alert(), rendered modal
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 17.3d complete
- **Key changes to AdminNotices.tsx**:
  - Added import: `import { AlertModal } from '@/components/ui/AlertModal';`
  - Added state: `alertModal` with isOpen, title, message, variant fields
  - Replaced `alert('Failed to perform action. Please try again.')` with `setAlertModal({ isOpen: true, title: 'Action Failed', message: '...', variant: 'error' })`
  - Added AlertModal render at end of component JSX
- **Pattern for replacing alert() calls**:
  1. Add AlertModal import
  2. Add state: `const [alertModal, setAlertModal] = useState<{isOpen: boolean; title: string; message: string; variant: '...'}>(...)`
  3. Replace `alert('...')` with `setAlertModal({ isOpen: true, ... })`
  4. Add AlertModal render: `<AlertModal isOpen={alertModal.isOpen} onClose={() => setAlertModal({...alertModal, isOpen: false})} ... />`
- **Test results**: All 497 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions
- **Pre-existing TypeScript errors**: Only in cypress/e2e/scripts directories (not in src/)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - The handleNoticeAction catch block was the first of 5 alert() calls identified in Task 17.3a

## Iteration 134 - Task 17.3f: Replace all confirm() calls with ConfirmModal
- **What was implemented**: Replaced all 2 confirm() calls with ConfirmModal component
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/pages/admin/AdminNotices.tsx` - Imported ConfirmModal, added state, replaced confirm(), added handler, rendered modal
  - `src/pages/admin/AccountManagement.tsx` - Imported ConfirmModal, added state, replaced confirm(), added handler, rendered modal
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 17.3f complete
- **Confirm calls replaced**:
  1. AdminNotices.tsx:137 `confirm('Are you sure you want to delete this notice?')` → ConfirmModal with danger variant
  2. AccountManagement.tsx:379 `confirm('Are you sure you want to delete this account?...')` → ConfirmModal with danger variant
- **Pattern for replacing confirm() calls**:
  1. Add ConfirmModal import
  2. Add state: `const [confirmModal, setConfirmModal] = useState<{isOpen: boolean; itemId: string | null}>({ isOpen: false, itemId: null })`
  3. Replace `if (confirm('...')) { doAction() }` with `setConfirmModal({ isOpen: true, itemId }); return;`
  4. Create async handler: `const handleConfirmDelete = async () => { ... supabase call ... setConfirmModal(closed) ... fetchData() }`
  5. Add ConfirmModal render with onClose, onConfirm={handleConfirmDelete}, title, message, confirmText, variant="danger"
- **Key implementation details**:
  - Both use danger variant (red styling) for destructive delete actions
  - Both have async handleConfirmDelete functions with try/catch error handling
  - Error handling shows AlertModal with error variant on failure
  - State includes itemId to track which item is being deleted
  - Return early after opening modal to prevent refresh until confirmation
- **Test results**: All 497 tests pass, no regressions
- **Verification**: `grep -n "confirm(" src/pages/admin/*.tsx` returns no results
- **All browser dialogs now replaced**: Task 17.3 (Replace Browser Alerts) is complete
  - alert() calls: 5 replaced with AlertModal (Task 17.3d + 17.3e)
  - confirm() calls: 2 replaced with ConfirmModal (Task 17.3f)
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - ConfirmModal has onConfirm callback for async operations
  - Pattern: open modal → return early → handle in callback → close modal → refresh data
  - Always include error handling that falls back to AlertModal
  - State shape: { isOpen: boolean, itemId: string | null } where itemId tracks the target
  - Next task is 17.4a: Identify broken edit/save in Settings page

## Iteration 139 - Tasks 17.5b-c: Create admin stats API endpoint
- **What was implemented**: Created admin stats API endpoint for dashboard real-time statistics
- **Files changed**:
  - `server/routes/admin/stats.ts` - NEW FILE (140 lines)
  - `server/index.ts` - Added import and route registration for adminStatsRouter
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Tasks 17.5b and 17.5c complete
- **Key features of admin stats endpoint**:
  - Uses `requireAdmin` middleware for authentication (checks is_platform_admin in JWT)
  - Returns real counts: totalCouncils, activeCouncils, totalFirms, totalNotices, totalUsers, pendingVerifications
  - Calculates **firm growth percentage**: compares firms created this month vs last month
  - Calculates **monthly revenue** based on correct pricing model:
    - Firms: £49/month subscription + £50/notice
    - Councils: £19.99/notice when publishing
    - Non-firm/council notices: £50/notice
  - Measures **API response time** by timing a health check query
  - Returns `systemHealth` status: 'healthy' (<1s), 'degraded' (1-5s), or 'down' (>5s)
  - Notes: databaseLoad and uptime return null (require external monitoring to implement)
- **API response structure**:
  ```json
  {
    "ok": true,
    "data": {
      "totalCouncils": 10,
      "activeCouncils": 8,
      "totalFirms": 5,
      "totalNotices": 100,
      "totalUsers": 25,
      "pendingVerifications": 2,
      "firmGrowthPercent": 15,
      "firmsThisMonth": 3,
      "firmsLastMonth": 2,
      "monthlyRevenue": 745.00,
      "firmSubscriptionRevenue": 245,
      "noticeRevenue": 500.00,
      "noticesThisMonth": 10,
      "systemHealth": "healthy",
      "apiResponseTime": 45,
      "databaseLoad": null,
      "uptime": null
    }
  }
  ```
- **Test results**: All 497 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions

## Iteration 140 - Task 17.5d: Create useAdminStats hook
- **What was implemented**: Created React Query hook for fetching admin dashboard stats
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/hooks/useAdminStats.ts` - NEW FILE (78 lines)
  - `PRD.md` - Marked task complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - Created `AdminStatsData` interface matching the API response structure
  - Interface includes: core counts (councils, firms, notices, users), growth metrics, revenue, system health
  - Uses 2-minute stale time (shorter than public useStats hook since admin dashboard is more sensitive)
  - Includes credential include for session auth
  - Custom retry logic: don't retry auth errors (401/403), retry other errors up to 3 times
  - Error messages for auth failures: 'Admin authentication required' and 'Admin access denied'
- **Test results**: All 498 tests pass (2 skipped), no regressions

- **Learnings for future iterations**:

  - Admin endpoints need `credentials: 'include'` for session cookie auth
  - Use shorter stale time for admin data that changes frequently
  - Add meaningful error messages for auth failures instead of generic errors

## Iteration 156 - Task 18.4a: Create UI.input style
- **What was implemented**: Added comprehensive input style exports to src/styles/ui.ts
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/styles/ui.ts` - Added 35 lines of input style exports with documentation
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 18.4a complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - `inputBase` - Base input style without width (h-11, rounded-xl, slate-300 border, blue-600/30 focus ring)
  - `inputFull` - Full-width version (`w-full ${inputBase}`)
  - `inputError` - Error state with rose-300 border, rose-50/30 background, rose-600 focus
  - `inputSmall` - Compact version (h-9, rounded-lg) for filters and inline forms
  - `textareaBase` - Multiline input with resize-y
  - `inputWithIconLeft`/`inputWithIconRight` - Base with pl-10/pr-10 for icon space
  - `inputIconLeft`/`inputIconRight` - Positioning classes for icons (absolute, centered vertically)
  - All inputs include disabled states (bg-slate-50, text-slate-500, cursor-not-allowed)
  - All inputs use transition-all duration-200 for smooth state changes
  - Focus uses blue-600/30 opacity ring (subtle, consistent with primary color)
- **Codebase patterns discovered**:
  - Many files use inline input styles with varying patterns (border-gray-300 vs border-slate-300)
  - Some use rounded-lg, others rounded-xl - standardized on rounded-xl for main inputs
  - Focus styles vary: some use focus:border-blue-500, others focus:ring-2 - standardized on both for consistency
  - Height varies from h-9 to h-11 - kept both as inputSmall (h-9) and inputBase (h-11)
- **Test results**: All 498 tests pass, 2 skipped
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors only (council/*.tsx, firm/Team.tsx, auth/Callback.tsx), no new errors in ui.ts
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Template literals can compose styles: `inputFull = \`w-full \${inputBase}\``
  - Including disabled states is important for form accessibility
  - The existing `input` export at line 107 is simpler but kept for backwards compatibility
  - New exports provide more comprehensive options (error state, small variant, icon positioning)
  - Next task is Task 18.4b: Create UI.select style

## Iteration 157 - Task 18.4b: Create UI.select style
- **What was implemented**: Added comprehensive select dropdown style exports to src/styles/ui.ts
- **Files changed**:
  - `src/styles/ui.ts` - Added 32 lines of select style exports with documentation
  - `PRD.md` - Marked Task 18.4b complete
- **Key implementation details**:
  - `selectBase` - Base select style without width (h-11, rounded-xl, slate-300 border, blue-600/30 focus ring, appearance-none, cursor-pointer)
  - `selectFull` - Full-width version (`w-full ${selectBase}`)
  - `selectError` - Error state with rose-300 border, rose-50/30 background, rose-600 focus
  - `selectSmall` - Compact version (h-9, rounded-lg, pr-8) for filters and inline forms
  - `selectChevron` - Icon positioning (absolute, right-3, centered vertically, h-4 w-4, pointer-events-none)
  - `selectChevronSmall` - Icon positioning for small selects (right-2)
  - All selects include disabled states (bg-slate-50, text-slate-500, cursor-not-allowed)
  - All selects use transition-all duration-200 for smooth state changes
  - Documented usage pattern: wrap select in relative container, add ChevronDown icon with selectChevron class
- **Codebase patterns discovered**:
  - Many files use inline select styles with varying patterns
  - Most selects use appearance-none with absolute-positioned chevron icon
  - pr-8/pr-10 padding needed to make room for chevron
  - Existing selects use rounded-lg; new standard uses rounded-xl for consistency with inputBase
- **Test results**: All 498 tests pass, 2 skipped
- **TypeScript check**: Pre-existing errors in cypress/, e2e/, scripts/ only; no new errors
- **Learnings for future iterations**:
  - Select styles follow same pattern as input styles (Base, Full, Error, Small variants)
  - Selects need appearance-none to hide native dropdown arrow, then custom chevron icon
  - pr-10 (selectBase) vs pr-8 (selectSmall) for different sized chevron spacing
  - Icon positioning uses pointer-events-none to allow clicks to pass through to select
  - Next task is Task 18.4c: Apply to admin forms
